# Engineering process — regulatory change and error prevention

Regulation changes constantly. This is the one route every change takes, and the log that turns every error into an
automatic guard. Improve both over time: each run of the route should be faster than the last, and no error should be
possible twice.

## 1. The regulatory-change route

| Step | What happens | Where |
|---|---|---|
| 1 Detect | CRCS finds the change: the EU register daily (dates, amending and replacing acts), and earlier, the regulators' own feeds and the press (unconfirmed signals). | `services/regulatory_monitoring/eurlex_detector.py`, `early_signals.py` |
| 2 Triage (≤ 1 day) | A person classifies the detected change: no impact · wording only · template change · new module. The decision is recorded on the change. | `reg_detected_change` |
| 3 Capture as a specification | Each version of a regulation's templates is a **versioned spec file**: templates, rows, columns, the instruction reference and a verbatim quote per element, the act (CELEX), its status (adopted / draft) and the dates it applies. Drafted from the official text by an agent, verified by a second pass. | `data/reference/regspec/<framework>/<version>.json` |
| 4 Diff | The machine compares the new spec with the one in force and lists what changed: templates, rows, columns added / removed / renamed, references moved. Unchanged cells carry over. | `services/regspec` |
| 5 Implement the diff only | The spec is the regulation only. Beside the code that fills it, a binding says how every row and column is filled: computed, input (a named per-loan fact the client supplies) or n/a. Titles, labels and legal citations are read from the spec, never typed in code. | `BINDING` (e.g. `services/governance/pillar3_grids.py`) |
| 6 Automatic checks | Coverage: every row and column of every adopted spec is bound, nothing bound is stale. A golden test book gives known answers under every adopted version (dual run). Each filing freezes the spec it was prepared under (version + file sha256) and is always rendered to it. | `tests/unit/test_regspec.py`, `test_pillar3_grids.py` |
| 7 Sign-off and release | A regulatory reviewer and an engineer — two different people — sign the spec file's exact sha256 (the database refuses the same person twice; editing the file voids earlier sign-offs). A filing built to an unsigned spec carries a warning on its run. Clients are told through the CRCS alerts. | `regspec_signoff`, operator page *Change pipeline* |
| 8 Retire | The previous version stays supported for restatements for six months after it stops applying, then is retired. | `reg_versions.LEGACY_SUPPORT_DAYS` |

A draft act (e.g. an EBA final draft ITS not yet adopted by the Commission) may be captured as a spec with status
`draft`: it is diffed and prepared, never used for a filing until adopted.

Where the official text is silent, the spec records a **declared reading** (`interpretations`: the reading, why, who
declared it) — never an unverified statement presented as the text. An adopted spec may not contain the word
UNVERIFIED (validation refuses it).

## 2. Before every commit — one command

```
scripts/precommit.sh
```

It runs, in order, and stops at the first failure: lint on the whole repository (as CI) · the migration graph check · the
migration round-trip (every revision up and down on a scratch database, exact schema restored) · the project documents match the code (`scripts/build_project_docs.py --check`: the four generated documents, the schema read from its own scratch build) · the web build · the full test suite (which ends with the demo-data guard) · the running-code check. Nothing is committed
until it passes.

## 3. Error log

Every error — found by a test, a review, a user or ourselves — gets a line here: what happened, the root cause, the fix,
and the **automatic guard** that makes it impossible (or at least loud) next time. A fix without a guard is not done.

| # | Date | What happened | Root cause | Automatic guard |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| E1 | 2026-09-28 | A "rolled-back" test renamed four demo sites for real. | The code under test called `session.commit()`; the test fixture only rolled back at the end. | `session_rolled_back` makes every commit a flush (`tests/integration/conftest.py`); `views.in_view` restores any override it replaces. |
| E2 | 2026-09-28 | Tests left 202 rows in an append-only table. | Committed tests cleaned their assets but not the new fact history. | Suite-wide demo-data guard: fingerprints the business tables and append-only counts before and after the run; any change fails the run and names the table (`tests/conftest.py`). |
| E3 | 2026-09-28 | A new module overwrote an unrelated existing file of the same name. | A file was written without checking the path was free. | Every module is imported by a test (`tests/unit/test_every_module_imports.py`); plus: check a path is free before creating a file. |
| E4 | 2026-09-28 | A live check ran against an API still serving the previous code. | The server was not restarted after a backend change. | `/health` reports the code version loaded; `scripts/check_running_code.sh` fails when it differs from the checkout. |
| E5 | 2026-09-28 | Security headers and metrics were silently off. | They sat in one try-block with a package that pyproject.toml never declared; a second, stale requirements file drifted. | Security headers always on (`api/security_headers.py`) and tested; one dependency list (pyproject.toml) checked against the environment (`tests/unit/test_environment_matches_requirements.py`); a missing optional package now logs a warning. |
| E6 | 2026-09-28 | Two helper agents were launched with unfilled placeholders in their instructions. | A prompt was written as a template and not completed. | Write every agent instruction in full — never with placeholders. |
| E7 | 2026-09-28 | Pillar 3 filings were prepared under an act the register shows replaced. | Template structure and its legal citation were typed into code; nothing compared them with the register. | Version pinning stamps and checks the act on every freeze; the regulatory-change route (spec files, diff, coverage) replaces hand-typed templates. |
| E8 | 2026-09-28 | A task-approval test left an approved request (and a finished task) behind on every run — 60 by the time the E2 guard caught it on its first run. | The test relied on a final rollback while the approval step inside it commits; its HTTP part cleaned the task but not the request. | The module's session makes commits flushes; the HTTP test deletes its own request; the E2 guard fails any recurrence. |
| E9 | 2026-09-28 | Pillar 3 Template 5 did not match the regulation: "chronic" included exposures sensitive to both; maturity and IFRS 9 columns covered all exposures, not the physical-risk-sensitive ones; rows were the sectors present, not the fixed 13; two impairment columns were missing. | The template was typed from a summary, not built from the instruction text. | The template is built from the verified spec (verbatim instructions); a golden book with hand-worked answers runs under every adopted version; coverage fails the build on any unmapped row or column. |
| E10 | 2026-09-28 | The XBRL element map cited Template 1 "Scope 1" and "Scope 2" columns that the template does not have. | References written from memory. | A test checks every template column the map cites exists in the governing spec. |
| E11 | 2026-09-28 | The pre-commit gate carried on after a lint failure. | `a && b || c` runs `c` when `b` fails. | Written as if / else; the gate stops at the first failure. |
| E12 | 2026-09-28 | A test found a form section by its title and broke when titles began to follow the regulation. | Tests keyed on display text. | Sections carry stable keys; tests find them by key. |
| E13 | 2026-09-29 | Two optional SFDR indicators did not match the regulation: 'non-recycled waste' was a % of total waste (the RTS asks tonnes per € million invested, attributed), and 'threatened species' carried Table 1 no. 7's wording. | A hand-kept catalogue of indicators, named from memory. | The opt-in catalogue is generated from the spec's rows (official number and wording); the aggregation is declared per row in the binding and tested. |
| E14 | 2026-09-29 | The adopted Table 2 / 3 indicators never appeared on the SFDR form. | The form looked up datapoints under keys nothing produced; no test rendered them. | The form emits a datapoint per adopted indicator; a golden-payload test renders them with their official row numbers. |
| E15 | 2026-09-29 | Sign-off tests failed once real sign-offs existed. | The tests signed the real spec files, so they depended on live data state. | They sign a private copy of a spec in a temporary folder; only the freeze and lineage tests read the real specs. |
| E16 | 2026-09-29 | Regulatory lists and citations still typed in code after two runs: NACE groupings in the Pillar 3 bindings, the "high impact climate sectors" set, the EU member list, the collateral fallback, act numbers in the version register, outlook and supervisor profiles, the XBRL fact list twice. | Moving templates onto specs left the surrounding lists and texts where they were. | Groupings are read from the spec layout; regulatory sets and declarations live in cited reference data (`nace_sector_sets.json`, `declarations/`, EU membership from Eurostat GISCO); watched acts and titles come from specs plus `crcs/tracked_acts.json`; draft outlook entries are generated from draft specs and their diff; supervisor citations are read from the framework references; the XBRL map file is the one fact list, enforced by a test. |
| E17 | 2026-09-29 | Qualitative Tables 2 and 3 were typed with 13 and 4 rows; the regulation prints 17 and 16 (the (d)(i)-(iv) and (c)/(d)(i)-(vi) items were missing). The Template 10 editor was a free-form list with invented fields; the regulation prints 11 fixed rows. | Templates transcribed by hand. | Both are rendered from the verified spec; answers are keyed by the printed row; a save must name a row of the governing tables. |
| E18 | 2026-09-29 | Values typed into a template cell lived in one org-wide overlay — not tied to a reporting period, not frozen into the filing, not in its exports — and any cell could be typed over, including totals built from loan data. | Two parallel mechanisms (manual cells, provided datapoints), neither period-aware. | One mechanism (provided_data): a supplied value carries its reporting period, supersedes only the same period, is frozen into that period's filing; only cells the binding marks 'input:cell' accept one, validated against the governing spec. Tests: `test_supplied_cells.py`. |
| E19 | 2026-09-29 | A filing was recorded for "last calendar year-end" while its snapshot used the organisation's configured reporting period. | Three hard-coded period dates. | One source, `filings.reporting_period_end()`, used by the obligation calendar, pre-flight, generation and the freeze. |
| E20 | 2026-09-29 | A GAR total that names a total printed below it (Template 7 row 1 → row 2) read it before it was computed. | Totals were computed in printed order. | Totals resolve by dependency (cycle-checked); the golden book covers it (`test_pillar3_gar.py`). |
| E21 | 2026-09-29 | Removing a code block by "up to the next blank lines" deleted unrelated models and helpers. | An unbounded cut. | Cut between exact start and end markers and read the diff stat before continuing; lint (undefined names) fails the gate. Recurred 2026-09-29 (retiring gar_grid: a module constant and a test helper sat between the cut markers); lint (undefined names) and the tests caught both before commit, as designed — after a cut, list the top-level definitions the diff removes. |
| E22 | 2026-09-29 | The GAR engine read a Taxonomy status of "eligible" as "assessed, not aligned", so a book classified to eligibility only would report a GAR of 0 %. | The classifier's own contract (it never returns "aligned"; eligibility only) was not carried into the consumer. | Alignment is known only where it is stated (an aligned status or the client's CCM-sustainable fact); otherwise the aligned columns are blank and the form says how many eligible exposures lack an alignment determination. Golden-book assertion in `test_pillar3_gar.py`. |
| E23 | 2026-09-29 | A capture agent handed back before the agents it had started finished; their work was lost with it and no spec file was written. | A coordinator agent spawned nested workers and could not wait for them. | Capture workers are launched directly from the session, do their work themselves (no sub-agents) and hand back only when their files are written and verified; the session collects them. |
| E24 | 2026-09-29 | A record could be read by id across organisations without signing in (bank asset, insurance policy, supply plot and site, property, holding); fund writes accepted anonymous callers; six routers each had their own copy of the "which organisation" resolver (one pointed the supply demo at the REIT); refusals came back as 200 with an `error` field; refused voluntary-PAI values were dropped silently; the geocoding proxy and the heavy scores summary were open. | Organisation scoping lived in each router, and nothing checked the route table as a whole. | One resolver (`api.deps.tenant_resolver`) and one ownership check (`own_or_404`: another organisation's record is a 404, like a missing one); writes need a signed-in user with the permission; refusals are HTTP errors; refused values are reported back. Guards: `tests/unit/test_route_security.py` fails the build for any unauthenticated route not on the reviewed list (`api/public_routes.py`) or any anonymous id route without an ownership check; `tests/integration/test_e2e_tenant_isolation.py` proves it through the API. |
| E25 | 2026-09-29 | Migrations could not be undone faithfully: downgrades failed (a view cannot lose columns in place; a column other views still used) or "succeeded" but restored something different (types, checks, constraints, functions, views); 13 migrations read live application code (the hazard list, the ORM, a sync helper), so replaying history built today's shape, not theirs. | Downgrades were never run, and nothing required a migration to be self-contained. | Every migration is self-contained: lists and table definitions frozen into the file (vocabulary lists from git history; ORM-built tables as frozen DDL under `versions/sql/`); registry data is synced by the app at API and worker start, never by a migration. Guards: `tests/unit/test_migrations_self_contained.py` (no migration imports the application) and `scripts/check_migration_roundtrip.py` in the gate and CI — builds a scratch database one revision at a time, undoes each step and requires the exact prior schema, naming every defective downgrade. |
| E26 | 2026-09-29 | CI had been red since 2026-09-12 and nobody saw it: CI and the Docker image ran Python 3.9 while the code needed 3.11+ (tomllib, `int | None` in API models) and development ran 3.14 — the shipped image would not have started; 50 lint errors had accumulated; ruff was unpinned in CI. The migration round-trip added to CI never ran (its job was skipped behind the failing ones). | Three places declared the Python version and none was checked against the others; the local gate linted only changed files and never looked at CI. | One Python version declared in pyproject.toml (3.14) and used by CI and the image; `tests/unit/test_python_version.py` fails when any of them (or ruff's target, or the running interpreter) disagree. ruff pinned in the dev extra and CI installs that pin. The gate lints the whole repository, as CI does. After every push the CI result is read before the work is called done. The deploy job skips with a notice while no production cluster is connected (go-live dependency 14) instead of failing on every push. |
| E27 | 2026-09-29 | The Pillar 3 walkthrough found: an obligation card's "Prepare" passed only the framework, so an entity's solo obligation would be filed for the whole organisation; the pre-filing check showed the whole organisation's book (146 assets, EUR 4.16bn) while the filing froze one entity's (49, EUR 1.24bn), and its confirmation was not bound to the scope; an obligation for another period could be prepared into the configured period; a supervisory deadline turned a typed "2026-Q3" into an annual period labelled as a quarter (41 obligations inherited it); cards for different entities looked identical; the dialog could not be closed with Escape and its close button had no name. | The obligation, the pre-filing check and the freeze each decided the scope on their own; period labels were typed, not derived; each overlay was built by hand. | Preparing an obligation sends it; the server files exactly its entity and refuses another period (`_obligation_scope`). The pre-filing check computes on the same book the freeze uses (`_book_scope`) and its token binds the scope; one scope check (`_check_scope`) serves both. Period labels are derived (`filings.period_label`), supervisory periods are financial years only (`canonical_period`), and the database enforces label = FY + year of the period end on filings, obligations and deadlines. Cards show the entity and role. One shared `Dialog` primitive. Tests: `test_e2e_pillar3_lifecycle.py` (obligation scope, period and scope-bound confirmation), `test_period_labels.py`, `test_supervision_deadlines.py`. The new label rule also exposed tests that typed labels, and a test that assumed a demo asset had never been recorded (true only until the app is used): tests derive labels and create the records they need inside their rolled-back transaction. |
| E28 | 2026-09-29 | Three sessions (this one and two suggested background tasks) worked in the same checkout at once: two new migrations branched from the same parent (two heads, which fails every gate), each session's gate tested the others' unfinished work, and a peer's `alembic upgrade head` applied this session's uncommitted migration to the live database. | Suggested tasks were started in the working checkout, not in worktrees of their own, and nothing coordinated migration order or staging. | Suggested-task prompts say to work in a git worktree of their own. While sessions do share a checkout: one migration chain (a new migration's parent is agreed, and chained after a peer's rather than beside it), each session stages only its own files (never `git add -A`), each gate runs on its own scratch database (`MIGCHECK_DB`), a failure in a peer's file is reported to that peer, not edited. `check_migrations` also fails a revision id longer than alembic_version's 32 characters. |
| E29 | 2026-09-29 | The Pillar 3 GAR (Templates 7-9) valued general-purpose lending to undertakings by the exposure's own stated Taxonomy status. The ITS says the GAR is the one of Delegated Regulation 2021/2178, disclosed once 'based on the turnover alignment of the counterparty for the general purpose lending part only' (Annex XL Template 8 para 4) — so the Pillar 3 and Taxonomy GARs of the same bank could differ. | Pillar 3 was built before the Taxonomy templates, each engine with its own valuation. | One valuation for both (`services/governance/taxonomy_valuation.py`): counterparty KPIs for general-purpose lending (turnover basis in Pillar 3), own status for specific-purpose, households and local government. Guard: `test_pillar3_and_the_taxonomy_disclose_one_gar`; the Pillar 3 golden book states counterparty KPIs. |
| E30 | 2026-09-29 | The Taxonomy classifier marked every NACE 35.11 exposure (electricity generation) eligible under a non-existent 'Annex I §4', from a two-entry NACE table typed in code; the recompute script overwrote every bank's stated Taxonomy status with that reading (client data included) and wrote sector names ('Hospitality') into the activity field; the demo drew each eligible exposure's objective at random. | No reference list of the Taxonomy's activities existed, so eligibility was typed, and the script had no scope. | The activity list captured from the Delegated Acts (2021/2139 + 2022/1214 + 2023/2485, and 2023/2486 — 242 activities, independently second-passed, `data/reference/taxonomy/activities.json`, `services/reference/taxonomy_activities.py`). A NACE code decides the activity only when every activity listing it is the same activity; otherwise 'not determined' with the candidates (counted as not assessed). The recompute script touches demo books only and never writes a sector as an activity; the demo objective follows the activity. Guard: `tests/unit/test_taxonomy_activities.py`. |

The 40 published fits adopted as recipes with the production source the ledger records; re-run: the same 8 pass the downside gate, upside passes Wheat MA and Olive oil ES (as the agreed test), 26 reproduce exactly, 14 are corrected under today's cycle rule — none crosses the bar. Publication only through 'calibration.publish' (system proposes at one approver; calibration.review decides): the fit names its recipe, run, yield series and cycle rule; the calibration row follows its own driver's published fit (season, box, baseline years). Seasons from data/reference/crop_seasons.json: MIRCA-OS 2015 by one share rule (months holding half the area, one unbroken run, harvest-year assignment — India wheat held: several calendars), FAO-56 Table 11 rows for olive, citrus and wine grapes applied to Mediterranean-coast countries only (page kept, rows checked against it; almonds held: no row). Registry: crop_map and season_source per crop. Recipes generated for every crop × origin with a national series ≥ 18 years and a season (drought SPEI-6, the protocol's one pre-registered driver); every other origin recorded with its reason (crop_calibration_coverage). Heat standardised by the 1991–2020 seasons. Box and crop-area recipes read the build (soil water keeps its regional file — the global files hold no soil moisture). A re-run with the same figures as a pending run adds nothing; other figures replace it; a batch publishes the runs still pending. ERA5 fetch: full years to last year plus the current year's months (Copernicus constraints, no download to check), one fetch at a time; feed weather_era5_land_global queues job calibration.refresh_weather (fetch → build → generate → re-run → propose). Guards: tests/integration/test_crop_area_weather.py, test_calibration_pipeline. |

| E171 | 2026-10-06 | Building the loss engine on the E169 model exposed three traps a loss figure would have fallen into unseen. (1) The published global cyclone curves (Eberenz et al. 2021) read the '1 min sustained wind speed' (page 395) while STORM states its wind as a 10-minute mean ('Vmax10'), so applying them would mis-state every cyclone loss by the averaging gap. (2) STORM's tracks reach Europe far more often than observed ones (Madrid: 15% of simulated years within 400 km against 3 of 46 observed seasons) because its basins run to 60°N/S with no extratropical transition. (3) FEMA's waterDepth is partly in feet (median waterDepth/exteriorWaterDepth 0.083 over 219,532 claims), and the measured damage means fall between two depth bands. (4) The engine's first cut multiplied the building damage ratio by each book's stated value — a property's market value and a loan's appraised collateral (both with the land), a holding's position — though the measured ratio is building damage over the building's own value; only insurance schedules state a building value (all 1,205 policies). (5) 238 assets state 'EU' as their country (a grouping, ref_countries.is_country = false, E154), so a curve chosen by country would have been looked up for 'EU'. | A curve, a layer, a claim field and a book value were each taken as what their name said, with nothing comparing the curve's stated metric to the layer's, nothing limiting a layer to where its source is sound, and no pre-registered test deciding whether a measured curve may give a figure. | services/loss: intensity.py samples the JRC river-flood depths and the STORM-derived wind (storm_events.py: annual maximum per synthetic year, quantile per return period) into hazard_intensity_samples through the isolated reader; measured.py builds the US residential flood curve from 273,373 NFIP claims on exteriorWaterDepth (data/reference/loss_protocol.json, registered before the test) with bands pooled where the mean falls, and tests it on 38 held-out events — band coverage 0.779 passes, event totals (11 of 38 within ±30%) fail, so its figures are 'held'; curves.py refuses a family whose metric no layer measures (both Eberenz families, until a cited 10→1-minute conversion is registered) and limits a family to the countries it names; engine.py walks every asset and peril through one decision tree (adopted family → stated or crosswalked occupancy → country → value → samples) to a range p10/central/p90 with its standing (published, held, untested) or a named gap, the expected annual loss as the area under the loss–exceedance curve, and a book curve only from published figures on the stated dependence (migration loss_band_20261006: range and standing on loss_results). The institution adopts a family by a stated-method election (loss_curve_family_flood/storm, loss_flood_dependence). A family's ratio is taken only of the value its damage basis names: migration loss_value_20261006 gives the property and loan books the building's own value (land excluded) and every upload template gains it and the building use (occupancy, the curve classes only); without it the loss is a named gap ('the property value includes the land'), and a holding has none. A grouping as country is a named gap with the country the coordinates fall in as a hint, never a substitute. The output: /v1/loss (families with their test or why refused, layers, runs with totals per standing — never added across standing — gaps by reason and per-asset ranges; job loss.run) and Review → Loss in the web app; a switch with nothing usable says so in Settings → Methodology. Guard: tests/unit/test_loss_curves.py (metric mismatch and missing countries refused; a measured family carries derivation, test and bands), tests/integration/test_loss_engine.py (every missing input a named gap; a range with low ≤ central ≤ high and the curve's standing; EAL = area under the curve; book curve only on published figures and a stated dependence; the building value, never the market value; a grouping as country named), tests/integration/test_loss_api.py, tests/integration/test_intake_every_sector.py::test_building_use_and_value_land_where_the_loss_engine_reads_them, ck_loss_results_standing / ck_loss_results_range in the schema. |
| E172 | 2026-10-06 | Four defects in data and reads the platform already relied on, found building the 30 m flood refinement. (1) The Copernicus EMS catalogue lists, under Storm Boris (EMSR756, AOI 2 Jelenia Góra, product v2), a layer of another activation (EMSR765_AOI01, a flood in Bolivia); the fetch trusted every listed link, so the Boris extent held three Bolivian polygons, 90.3% of its area. The flood validator barely moved (it reads nodes inside the event box: Boris ρ 0.394 → 0.390), but the terrain fetch planned from the extent's bounds — 5,883 tiles, about 235 GB — and was stopped at 76 tiles (2.5 GB, all southern hemisphere, deleted). (2) The validator took the observed polygons' convex hull as the area EMS mapped, so with several activations (Boris: Poland, Czechia, Slovakia; the July 2021 flood: Germany, Belgium, the Netherlands) the unmapped land between them counted as observed dry; the refetch also finds EMSR468 (Storm Alex in Italy) and EMSR518/520 (Belgium, the Netherlands) that the 2026-09-07 fetch missed. (3) The isolated reader's 'masked' read (E170) marked a nodata pixel as outside the shape: a dry pixel of the flood maps is nodata, so a box of dry land read as no land, and a forest tile with a nodata value would have counted it as loss. (4) The 30 m refinement met its registered rule by +0.0015 in rank correlation and +0.0009 in AUC — inside sampling noise. (5) The two new loss migrations' refusal probes borrowed an existing organisation, so on the empty round-trip database they planted nothing; and the applied-migration guard (E100) hashed the whole file, so correcting a probe (a fixture that never runs on upgrade or downgrade) on the development database would have meant deleting recorded loss results to downgrade. | An input was taken for what its label said: a catalogue link for the product's own layer, a hull for the mapped area, a nodata mask for the shape, an extent's bounds for the area a test reads; a pass rule had no noise margin; and a test fixture was fingerprinted as if it were schema. | scripts/fetch_ems_flood_footprints.py accepts a layer only when its file names the activation and AOI it is listed under and its polygons lie inside that product's outline (refused layers recorded with the reason) and records the mapped area (the accepted products' outlines); `--refetch` rebuilds named events only; all six refetched (Boris 29 layers, 1 refused). The validator judges a node only where at least half its box was mapped, on the flooded share of the mapped part: pooled ρ 0.68, AUC 0.80 on 144 nodes (was ρ 0.47 on 417 counting unmapped land as dry). raster_sampler.masked returns 'inside' (the shape) and 'valid' (holding a value); the forest reader takes loss and tree cover from valid pixels only — readings identical on the 7 reference plots. scripts/fetch_glo30.py fetches the tiles under the events' boxes first (19 tiles, 0.62 GB), prices the plan before fetching and refuses above the approved volume; asset tiles are refused until the refinement is adopted. The refinement (services/loss/refine30.py, rule in loss_protocol.json) is recorded as a candidate with its paired-bootstrap interval (ρ −0.002 to +0.005, AUC −0.001 to +0.004): passing is necessary, adopting it a stated decision; a later refinement must clear that noise margin (registered 2026-10-06). The probes create their own organisation; core.db.migration_fingerprint records, beside the file hash, a fingerprint of the migration without its REFUSAL_PROBE (migration_meta.applied_file.schema_sha256, backfilled for every row whose file still matches), and the applied check accepts an edit confined to the probe — any other edit still fails. Guard: tests/unit/test_ems_extents.py (a layer of another activation or outside its outline refused; every landed extent records its layers, refusals and mapped area, its flood inside it; terrain plan priced and capped; asset tiles wait), tests/unit/test_flood_jrc.py::test_a_node_is_judged_only_where_ems_mapped_it, tests/unit/test_raster_sampler.py::test_masked_tells_inside_the_shape_from_holding_a_value, tests/unit/test_refine30.py (the stated re-spread; the 90 m reading is the validator's), registry check: a refinement in use needs a passed test, tests/unit/test_migration_fingerprint.py (a probe-only edit keeps the fingerprint; any other edit changes it), the round-trip check (each probe proves its refusal). |
